prompt

25 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified raygentop under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 4.84712 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 6 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 4.7578e+06 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 2.30616e+07. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in matmult: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into a flip-flop (a product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.42 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out; 4.869 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in a module not established, is no multiply, a register through two carry chains (8 stages, then 4) and two LUTs into a flip-flop; wire 3.75 ns in all, 1.21 of it into the first chain, 4.565 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Wire and adder delays measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block, given as a range with the median in parentheses. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, the nine products on three tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. raygentop.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349), g ($351,$352), b ($354,$355)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 7
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,ru}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1r+i2r); chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,gu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,gv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1g+i2g); chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,bu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,bv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1b+i2b); chainout unused.
    (u, v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858; ru,rv,gu,gv,bu,bv are the module's 7-bit inputs, taken unregistered — the block's own input registers replace ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl.)

PLAN:

What the region is. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) three colour sums are built identically. At each posedge clk (raygentop.v:2860-2883, no reset in the block) the operands are registered (ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=255-u-v, rul<=ru …), and at the same edge the nine products are registered from the previous values of those registers (i1r<=ul*rul …). The outputs are combinational and truncated to 7 bits: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r 7 bits wide (raygentop.v:2884-2886), so only the low 7 bits of each 15-bit product sum are observable. ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl and i1*,i2* are read nowhere else — only inside those sums — so they may go inside a block. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → temp_shadedata*, and blb,blg → linearmap), so they are reproduced bit-exactly outside.

What is converted, and what is left.

  • Converted (6 regions): the two u/v products of each colour, $348+$349 → one block for r, $351+$352 → one block for g, $354+$355 → one block for b. Each block computes u*ru + v*rv (etc.) exactly — the block's ax*ay+bx*by with chainin=0.
  • Left (3 regions): $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353 (i3g<=wl*gwl), $356 (i3b<=wl*bwl). Reason, shared by all three: a block holds only two products, and the third product of a sum cannot be chained into the same stage. The chain costs a cycle — chainout of block 1 is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches its result two edges later, so a two-block chain puts the final sum 3 edges after the operands, while the design puts r/g/b 2 edges after u/ru. A chain would therefore delay r,g,b by one cycle and break the comparison. Giving each leftover product its own unchained block would need 3 more blocks (3 more DSP tiles) to save three 7-bit adds — a clear area loss. So they stay as they are: three wl* products in soft-registered form on one DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and one 7-bit soft add per colour recombines them.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. The nine products sat on 3 tiles; the three remaining wl* products fit one tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt. So bilinearintrp goes 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7 of the 132 available: +253779 MWTA, about 9.1 CLBs' worth.

Why this still lowers area × delay. The design's critical path is a hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, which this rewrite does not touch, so the period stays at the baseline 4.84712 ns and the objective moves only with area. Against the +1 tile the rewrite frees, per colour, the two 7-bit operand registers (rul,rvl etc. — flip-flop-only BLEs fed straight from module inputs), the two product registers i1*,i2*, and one of the two adder levels of the three-input sum; ul,vl disappear as well (their mux LUTs stay, so those BLEs do not free). That is roughly 35 BLEs per colour if synthesis has already pruned the products and adders to the 7 live bits, and about 59 per colour if it kept them 15 bits wide — 10.5 to 17.7 CLBs, i.e. 293k to 494k MWTA, against the 254k the extra tile costs. Expected area change −0.8 % to −5 %, delay unchanged. This is the best split available: converting any smaller set still costs at least one extra tile (the leftovers do not pack into fewer), and converting all nine needs 6 blocks (products of different colour sums cannot share a block) for +3 tiles, which the soft savings do not cover.

Stages.

  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block drives another; every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected. The block's own stage is input register → output register = 2.219 ns, well under the 4.84712 ns critical stage.
  • Stage ending at a block input: source register (0.060) → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 1.9 ns worst case. The ru…bv operands come from shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so those are I/O paths and untimed. Not the slowest stage.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft adder level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. The baseline path to the same flip-flops carried one adder level more, so this stage shortens by about 1 ns; it was not the slowest stage before and is not now.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the r/g/b output stage, one of the two adder levels per colour; out of the input stages, the ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl registers and the i1*,i2* product registers. Neither stage is the slowest; the slowest stage (matmult) is untouched, which is why STAGE_NS is the baseline value and the gain is in area.
  • Register stages, converted regions, before: operand register (ul,rul) then product register (i1r) — 2 stages from u/ru to the value entering the sum. After: the block's input register then its result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them. Cycle alignment is identical: operands present before edge k give i1r+i2r after edge k+1 in the original and result after edge k+1 in the block. The left regions keep their 2 stages (wl/rwl register, then i3*), so result[6:0] and i3* are valid on the same cycle and the final 7-bit add is exact modulo 2^7, matching the original truncation.
  • Reset and power-up: bilinearintrp has no reset — its registers load unconditionally every edge — so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks, which makes the block load every edge too. The block's registers start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench applies 8 zero lead-in cycles before comparing, and the design has no path that reads r,g,b meaningfully before then.

Edits for the editor (only in bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2887; do not add a module definition for int_sop_2 — the primitive/behavioural model is supplied):

  1. Delete the declarations of ul, vl, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl, i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2796-2797, 2799-2807 partly, 2808-2816 partly). Keep u, v (combinational), wl, rwl, gwl, bwl, i3r, i3g, i3b.
  2. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) keep only wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v;, rwl <= rw;, gwl <= gw;, bwl <= bw;, i3r <= wl * rwl;, i3g <= wl * gwl;, i3b <= wl * bwl;. Delete the other assignments.
  3. Add wire [36:0] sop_r_result, sop_g_result, sop_b_result; and three unused wire [36:0] sop_*_chainout;, then the three instances with the port connections given in MAPPING (connect by name; every input port connected, chainin 37'b0, mode_sigs 12'b0, reset 1'b0).
  4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:
    assign r = sop_r_result[6:0] + i3r[6:0];
    assign g = sop_g_result[6:0] + i3g[6:0];
    assign b = sop_b_result[6:0] + i3b[6:0];
    (7-bit operands into a 7-bit target: the carry out is dropped, exactly as the original 15-bit sum truncated to r's 7 bits.)
  5. Change nothing else in the file; no other module is touched.

No assumption about the design's inputs is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit colours into 19-bit ay/by), each product is at most 255·127 and the pair sum at most 64770, far below 2^37, so the block's wrap never occurs and the low 7 bits always agree with the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if bilinearintrp's r,g,b were allowed one extra cycle of latency, the third product of each colour could be chained (chainout→chainin) instead of added in soft logic, removing all three soft adders — but at 6 blocks/6 tiles instead of 4, so it is reported only as the change that would let the remaining three candidates use the block, not as an area win.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349,$350), g ($351,$352,$353), b ($354,$355,$356)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.57484
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(ru-rw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(rv-rw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; r = result[6:0] - rwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; g = result[6:0] - gwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; b = result[6:0] - bwl2; chainout unused.
    (u,v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace ul,vl. ru…bw are the module's 7-bit inputs; each difference is a 7-bit wrapping subtract in soft logic, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl. rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 are rw,gw,bw delayed two stages.)

PLAN:

What changes against the kept candidate. The kept rewrite put six of the nine products in three blocks and left the three wl* products on a fourth DSP tile, so bilinearintrp went 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7; area fell only 0.53 %. This revision folds the third product of each colour into the same two block products algebraically, so all nine candidate products disappear from soft/DSP multiply logic, bilinearintrp uses 3 tiles (the three blocks) and the design is back at the baseline's 6 tiles. The extra 253779 MWTA tile of the kept candidate is given back, and the wl register, the rwl/gwl/bwl-fed products, the i3* product registers and the 8-bit 255-u-v chain go away as well; the cost is three 7-bit delay registers and three 7-bit input subtracts.

The algebra (exact, not an approximation). In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) all registers load at one posedge clk (2860-2883, no reset): ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'b11111111-u-v, rul<=ru, rwl<=rw …, and at the same edge i1r<=ul*rul, i2r<=vl*rvl, i3r<=wl*rwl. The outputs are combinational and 7 bits wide: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r declared [6:0] (2884-2886, 2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits are observable. Therefore, with every signal taken at the same register stage,

r ≡ ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl (mod 128).

wl is the 8-bit wrap of 255−u−v, and a mod-256 value is congruent mod 128, so wl·rwl ≡ (255−ul−vl)·rwl (mod 128), giving

r ≡ 255·rwl + ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) ≡ ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) − rwl (mod 128),

since 255·rwl ≡ 127·rwl ≡ −rwl (mod 128). The two remaining products are exactly ax*ay + bx*by. The differences are fed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts, which are congruent to the true differences mod 128; because a factor congruent mod 128 changes the product only by a multiple of 128 (u ≤ 255 so u·2⁷·k is a multiple of 128), and because u·(ru−rw mod 128) + v·(rv−rw mod 128) ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2³⁷ so the block never wraps, result[6:0] equals (ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl)) mod 128 exactly. The final 7-bit subtract of the delayed rw closes it. Same reasoning for g and b. No ASSUMES: line is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit differences into 19-bit ay/by).

Check on the testbench's impulse (tb_equiv_bilin.v:173, all inputs 1, selectuv=1): reference ul=vl=1, wl=253, r=(1+1+253) mod 128=127; rewrite result=1·0+1·0=0, rwl2=1, r=(0−1) mod 128=127. ✓

Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl,i1*,i2*,i3* are module-local and read only inside these three sums (they are declared at 2794-2816 and used only at 2862-2886; the ul/vl at 2752-2756 are different registers in a different module). Nothing outside can observe them, so they may be absorbed. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → blr,blg,blb → the temp_shadedata* case muxes at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484, and blb,blg → linearmap, 2011); they are reproduced bit-exactly.

Every candidate region. All nine are converted, in three groups of three, one group per colour sum; none is left. The three that the kept candidate left — $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353, $356 — are converted not by giving them a block port (a block holds only two products, and chaining is impossible here, see below) but by substituting wl = 255−u−v so that their contribution becomes part of the two products of the same block plus a 7-bit constant-times-signal correction outside it. Their multiplies disappear from the design.

Why not chaining. A second block per colour taking the first's chainout would put the sum 3 edges after the operands (chainout is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches result two edges later), while the design produces r,g,b 2 edges after the operands; it would also cost 3 more tiles. The algebraic fold gives the same result with no extra latency and no extra tile, so every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

Blocks and tiles. 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. bilinearintrp held nine products on 3 tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt; it now holds no multiply at all, so it is 3 tiles of blocks: design total 6 of 132, the baseline count. Against the kept candidate this removes one DSP tile (−253779 MWTA ≈ 9 CLBs).

Soft logic, by stage, and the slowest stage.

  • Slowest stage: untouched. The measured critical path of the kept build (4.57484 ns at seed 1) runs from a $dffe~387 through a wide soft adder array (cell_hard_block$…, stride-17 adder rows) and three LUTs into another block's register — a soft-mapped wide multiply, not in bilinearintrp (this module's multiplies are all on DSPs/blocks). This rewrite changes nothing on it, so STAGE_NS is that figure; the baseline's own slowest stage (the two_mult_18x19 hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, 4.869 on the baseline report) is likewise untouched.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ax/bx: source register → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v (2818-2858) → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 0.060+0.18+1.419+0.019 ≈ 1.7 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ay/by: ru…bw are shadedata[*] = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so the added 7-bit subtract sits on an I/O path, which is not timed. The only timed logic it could meet is none. Not the slowest.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft subtract level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. Depth is the same as the kept candidate's 7-bit add and one adder level less than the baseline's two 15-bit chains. Not the slowest.
  • New reg-to-reg stage rwl → rwl2 (and gwl,bwl): clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire + setup ≈ 1.4 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the stage that ended at the old operand/product registers — ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl,wl and the 8-bit 255−u−v carry chain, plus the i1*,i2*,i3* product registers (the rwl,gwl,bwl registers stay and gain a second rank); out of the output stage — both 15-bit add chains per colour, replaced by one 7-bit subtract. Neither stage is the slowest, which is why the gain is expected in area, with the period staying where the kept build put it.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block, 2.219 ns (input register → output register), well under 4.575 ns. No block drives another.

Register stages, per converted region. Before: operand register (ul/rul, vl/rvl, wl/rwl) then product register (i1r,i2r,i3r) — 2 stages from u,v,ru,rv,rw to the value entering the sum, and r combinational after that. After: block input register then block result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them; the −rw correction is matched by the 2-rank rw→rwl→rwl2 delay, so it is also 2 stages and r is still combinational on top. Operands present before edge k give r after edge k+1 in both designs; no cycle moves.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and its registers load unconditionally, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks and they load at every edge, matching. The block registers and the new rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench's 8 zero lead-in cycles precede any comparison (tb_equiv_bilin.v:217-219), and with zero inputs the defined value is 0, the reference's value.

Expected effect. Area: one DSP tile less than the kept candidate (−253779) and a net loss of soft state (wl 8 FF, i1*,i2*,i3* product registers, the 8-bit subtract chain, one 15-bit add chain per colour go; 21 FF of second-rank w delay and 21 BLEs of input subtract come), so roughly 4.45e6–4.55e6 MWTA against the kept 4.73253e6 and the baseline 4.7578e6. Delay: the slowest stage is untouched, so the period should stay near the kept build's 4.57484 ns; at that period the objective lands near 2.04e7–2.08e7, below the kept 2.16506e7 and about 10 % below the baseline's 2.30616e7 — a structural tile saving, larger than the ±4.6 % placement-seed noise on this design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the measured critical path is a wide soft-mapped multiply outside bilinearintrp (matmult's am11…am33, raygentop.v:335-343, of which only some fit the DSPs in two_mult_18x19 mode); pairing those products onto int_sop_2 blocks — or raising the flow's hard-multiplier budget so none is soft-mapped — is what would shorten the design's slowest stage. Those products are not among the candidate regions offered here.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, the nine products on three tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. raygentop.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349), g ($351,$352), b ($354,$355)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 7
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,ru}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1r+i2r); chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,gu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,gv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1g+i2g); chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,bu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,bv}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; result[6:0] replaces (i1b+i2b); chainout unused.
    (u, v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858; ru,rv,gu,gv,bu,bv are the module's 7-bit inputs, taken unregistered — the block's own input registers replace ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl.)

PLAN:

What the region is. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) three colour sums are built identically. At each posedge clk (raygentop.v:2860-2883, no reset in the block) the operands are registered (ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=255-u-v, rul<=ru …), and at the same edge the nine products are registered from the previous values of those registers (i1r<=ul*rul …). The outputs are combinational and truncated to 7 bits: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r 7 bits wide (raygentop.v:2884-2886), so only the low 7 bits of each 15-bit product sum are observable. ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl and i1*,i2* are read nowhere else — only inside those sums — so they may go inside a block. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → temp_shadedata*, and blb,blg → linearmap), so they are reproduced bit-exactly outside.

What is converted, and what is left.

  • Converted (6 regions): the two u/v products of each colour, $348+$349 → one block for r, $351+$352 → one block for g, $354+$355 → one block for b. Each block computes u*ru + v*rv (etc.) exactly — the block's ax*ay+bx*by with chainin=0.
  • Left (3 regions): $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353 (i3g<=wl*gwl), $356 (i3b<=wl*bwl). Reason, shared by all three: a block holds only two products, and the third product of a sum cannot be chained into the same stage. The chain costs a cycle — chainout of block 1 is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches its result two edges later, so a two-block chain puts the final sum 3 edges after the operands, while the design puts r/g/b 2 edges after u/ru. A chain would therefore delay r,g,b by one cycle and break the comparison. Giving each leftover product its own unchained block would need 3 more blocks (3 more DSP tiles) to save three 7-bit adds — a clear area loss. So they stay as they are: three wl* products in soft-registered form on one DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and one 7-bit soft add per colour recombines them.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. The nine products sat on 3 tiles; the three remaining wl* products fit one tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt. So bilinearintrp goes 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7 of the 132 available: +253779 MWTA, about 9.1 CLBs' worth.

Why this still lowers area × delay. The design's critical path is a hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, which this rewrite does not touch, so the period stays at the baseline 4.84712 ns and the objective moves only with area. Against the +1 tile the rewrite frees, per colour, the two 7-bit operand registers (rul,rvl etc. — flip-flop-only BLEs fed straight from module inputs), the two product registers i1*,i2*, and one of the two adder levels of the three-input sum; ul,vl disappear as well (their mux LUTs stay, so those BLEs do not free). That is roughly 35 BLEs per colour if synthesis has already pruned the products and adders to the 7 live bits, and about 59 per colour if it kept them 15 bits wide — 10.5 to 17.7 CLBs, i.e. 293k to 494k MWTA, against the 254k the extra tile costs. Expected area change −0.8 % to −5 %, delay unchanged. This is the best split available: converting any smaller set still costs at least one extra tile (the leftovers do not pack into fewer), and converting all nine needs 6 blocks (products of different colour sums cannot share a block) for +3 tiles, which the soft savings do not cover.

Stages.

  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block drives another; every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected. The block's own stage is input register → output register = 2.219 ns, well under the 4.84712 ns critical stage.
  • Stage ending at a block input: source register (0.060) → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 1.9 ns worst case. The ru…bv operands come from shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so those are I/O paths and untimed. Not the slowest stage.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft adder level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. The baseline path to the same flip-flops carried one adder level more, so this stage shortens by about 1 ns; it was not the slowest stage before and is not now.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the r/g/b output stage, one of the two adder levels per colour; out of the input stages, the ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl registers and the i1*,i2* product registers. Neither stage is the slowest; the slowest stage (matmult) is untouched, which is why STAGE_NS is the baseline value and the gain is in area.
  • Register stages, converted regions, before: operand register (ul,rul) then product register (i1r) — 2 stages from u/ru to the value entering the sum. After: the block's input register then its result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them. Cycle alignment is identical: operands present before edge k give i1r+i2r after edge k+1 in the original and result after edge k+1 in the block. The left regions keep their 2 stages (wl/rwl register, then i3*), so result[6:0] and i3* are valid on the same cycle and the final 7-bit add is exact modulo 2^7, matching the original truncation.
  • Reset and power-up: bilinearintrp has no reset — its registers load unconditionally every edge — so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks, which makes the block load every edge too. The block's registers start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench applies 8 zero lead-in cycles before comparing, and the design has no path that reads r,g,b meaningfully before then.

Edits for the editor (only in bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2887; do not add a module definition for int_sop_2 — the primitive/behavioural model is supplied):

  1. Delete the declarations of ul, vl, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl, i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2796-2797, 2799-2807 partly, 2808-2816 partly). Keep u, v (combinational), wl, rwl, gwl, bwl, i3r, i3g, i3b.
  2. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) keep only wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v;, rwl <= rw;, gwl <= gw;, bwl <= bw;, i3r <= wl * rwl;, i3g <= wl * gwl;, i3b <= wl * bwl;. Delete the other assignments.
  3. Add wire [36:0] sop_r_result, sop_g_result, sop_b_result; and three unused wire [36:0] sop_*_chainout;, then the three instances with the port connections given in MAPPING (connect by name; every input port connected, chainin 37'b0, mode_sigs 12'b0, reset 1'b0).
  4. Replace lines 2884-2886 with:
    assign r = sop_r_result[6:0] + i3r[6:0];
    assign g = sop_g_result[6:0] + i3g[6:0];
    assign b = sop_b_result[6:0] + i3b[6:0];
    (7-bit operands into a 7-bit target: the carry out is dropped, exactly as the original 15-bit sum truncated to r's 7 bits.)
  5. Change nothing else in the file; no other module is touched.

No assumption about the design's inputs is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit colours into 19-bit ay/by), each product is at most 255·127 and the pair sum at most 64770, far below 2^37, so the block's wrap never occurs and the low 7 bits always agree with the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if bilinearintrp's r,g,b were allowed one extra cycle of latency, the third product of each colour could be chained (chainout→chainin) instead of added in soft logic, removing all three soft adders — but at 6 blocks/6 tiles instead of 4, so it is reported only as the change that would let the remaining three candidates use the block, not as an area win.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349,$350), g ($351,$352,$353), b ($354,$355,$356)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.57484
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(ru-rw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(rv-rw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; r = result[6:0] - rwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; g = result[6:0] - gwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; b = result[6:0] - bwl2; chainout unused.
    (u,v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace ul,vl. ru…bw are the module's 7-bit inputs; each difference is a 7-bit wrapping subtract in soft logic, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl. rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 are rw,gw,bw delayed two stages.)

PLAN:

What changes against the kept candidate. The kept rewrite put six of the nine products in three blocks and left the three wl* products on a fourth DSP tile, so bilinearintrp went 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7; area fell only 0.53 %. This revision folds the third product of each colour into the same two block products algebraically, so all nine candidate products disappear from soft/DSP multiply logic, bilinearintrp uses 3 tiles (the three blocks) and the design is back at the baseline's 6 tiles. The extra 253779 MWTA tile of the kept candidate is given back, and the wl register, the rwl/gwl/bwl-fed products, the i3* product registers and the 8-bit 255-u-v chain go away as well; the cost is three 7-bit delay registers and three 7-bit input subtracts.

The algebra (exact, not an approximation). In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) all registers load at one posedge clk (2860-2883, no reset): ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'b11111111-u-v, rul<=ru, rwl<=rw …, and at the same edge i1r<=ul*rul, i2r<=vl*rvl, i3r<=wl*rwl. The outputs are combinational and 7 bits wide: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r declared [6:0] (2884-2886, 2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits are observable. Therefore, with every signal taken at the same register stage,

r ≡ ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl (mod 128).

wl is the 8-bit wrap of 255−u−v, and a mod-256 value is congruent mod 128, so wl·rwl ≡ (255−ul−vl)·rwl (mod 128), giving

r ≡ 255·rwl + ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) ≡ ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) − rwl (mod 128),

since 255·rwl ≡ 127·rwl ≡ −rwl (mod 128). The two remaining products are exactly ax*ay + bx*by. The differences are fed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts, which are congruent to the true differences mod 128; because a factor congruent mod 128 changes the product only by a multiple of 128 (u ≤ 255 so u·2⁷·k is a multiple of 128), and because u·(ru−rw mod 128) + v·(rv−rw mod 128) ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2³⁷ so the block never wraps, result[6:0] equals (ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl)) mod 128 exactly. The final 7-bit subtract of the delayed rw closes it. Same reasoning for g and b. No ASSUMES: line is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit differences into 19-bit ay/by).

Check on the testbench's impulse (tb_equiv_bilin.v:173, all inputs 1, selectuv=1): reference ul=vl=1, wl=253, r=(1+1+253) mod 128=127; rewrite result=1·0+1·0=0, rwl2=1, r=(0−1) mod 128=127. ✓

Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl,i1*,i2*,i3* are module-local and read only inside these three sums (they are declared at 2794-2816 and used only at 2862-2886; the ul/vl at 2752-2756 are different registers in a different module). Nothing outside can observe them, so they may be absorbed. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → blr,blg,blb → the temp_shadedata* case muxes at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484, and blb,blg → linearmap, 2011); they are reproduced bit-exactly.

Every candidate region. All nine are converted, in three groups of three, one group per colour sum; none is left. The three that the kept candidate left — $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353, $356 — are converted not by giving them a block port (a block holds only two products, and chaining is impossible here, see below) but by substituting wl = 255−u−v so that their contribution becomes part of the two products of the same block plus a 7-bit constant-times-signal correction outside it. Their multiplies disappear from the design.

Why not chaining. A second block per colour taking the first's chainout would put the sum 3 edges after the operands (chainout is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches result two edges later), while the design produces r,g,b 2 edges after the operands; it would also cost 3 more tiles. The algebraic fold gives the same result with no extra latency and no extra tile, so every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

Blocks and tiles. 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. bilinearintrp held nine products on 3 tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt; it now holds no multiply at all, so it is 3 tiles of blocks: design total 6 of 132, the baseline count. Against the kept candidate this removes one DSP tile (−253779 MWTA ≈ 9 CLBs).

Soft logic, by stage, and the slowest stage.

  • Slowest stage: untouched. The measured critical path of the kept build (4.57484 ns at seed 1) runs from a $dffe~387 through a wide soft adder array (cell_hard_block$…, stride-17 adder rows) and three LUTs into another block's register — a soft-mapped wide multiply, not in bilinearintrp (this module's multiplies are all on DSPs/blocks). This rewrite changes nothing on it, so STAGE_NS is that figure; the baseline's own slowest stage (the two_mult_18x19 hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, 4.869 on the baseline report) is likewise untouched.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ax/bx: source register → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v (2818-2858) → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 0.060+0.18+1.419+0.019 ≈ 1.7 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ay/by: ru…bw are shadedata[*] = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so the added 7-bit subtract sits on an I/O path, which is not timed. The only timed logic it could meet is none. Not the slowest.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft subtract level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. Depth is the same as the kept candidate's 7-bit add and one adder level less than the baseline's two 15-bit chains. Not the slowest.
  • New reg-to-reg stage rwl → rwl2 (and gwl,bwl): clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire + setup ≈ 1.4 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the stage that ended at the old operand/product registers — ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl,wl and the 8-bit 255−u−v carry chain, plus the i1*,i2*,i3* product registers (the rwl,gwl,bwl registers stay and gain a second rank); out of the output stage — both 15-bit add chains per colour, replaced by one 7-bit subtract. Neither stage is the slowest, which is why the gain is expected in area, with the period staying where the kept build put it.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block, 2.219 ns (input register → output register), well under 4.575 ns. No block drives another.

Register stages, per converted region. Before: operand register (ul/rul, vl/rvl, wl/rwl) then product register (i1r,i2r,i3r) — 2 stages from u,v,ru,rv,rw to the value entering the sum, and r combinational after that. After: block input register then block result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them; the −rw correction is matched by the 2-rank rw→rwl→rwl2 delay, so it is also 2 stages and r is still combinational on top. Operands present before edge k give r after edge k+1 in both designs; no cycle moves.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and its registers load unconditionally, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks and they load at every edge, matching. The block registers and the new rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench's 8 zero lead-in cycles precede any comparison (tb_equiv_bilin.v:217-219), and with zero inputs the defined value is 0, the reference's value.

Edits for the editor (only inside bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2887; do not add a module definition for int_sop_2, the primitive/behavioural model is supplied):

  1. Replace the declarations at 2796-2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1b,i2b,i3b, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1r,i2r,i3r, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl) with:

    verilog
           reg[6:0] rwl;  reg[6:0] gwl;  reg[6:0] bwl;
       reg[6:0] rwl2; reg[6:0] gwl2; reg[6:0] bwl2;

wire[6:0] rud = ru - rw; wire[6:0] rvd = rv - rw;
wire[6:0] gud = gu - gw; wire[6:0] gvd = gv - gw;
wire[6:0] bud = bu - bw; wire[6:0] bvd = bv - bw;

wire[36:0] sop_r_result, sop_g_result, sop_b_result;
wire[36:0] sop_r_chainout, sop_g_chainout, sop_b_chainout;

   Keep `reg[7:0] u;` and `reg[7:0] v;` (2794-2795) and the whole combinational mux block 2818-2858 unchanged.
2. Replace the body of the `always @(posedge clk)` block (2860-2883) with exactly:
```verilog
           rwl <= rw ; gwl <= gw ; bwl <= bw ;
           rwl2 <= rwl ; gwl2 <= gwl ; bwl2 <= bwl ;

  1. Instantiate the three blocks, connecting by name, every input port connected:

    verilog
           int_sop_2 sop_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
           .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, rud}), .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, rvd}),
           .chainin(37'b0), .result(sop_r_result), .chainout(sop_r_chainout));
       int_sop_2 sop_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
           .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, gud}), .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, gvd}),
           .chainin(37'b0), .result(sop_g_result), .chainout(sop_g_chainout));
       int_sop_2 sop_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
           .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, bud}), .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, bvd}),
           .chainin(37'b0), .result(sop_b_result), .chainout(sop_b_chainout));

  2. Replace 2884-2886 with:

    verilog
           assign r = sop_r_result[6:0] - rwl2 ;
       assign g = sop_g_result[6:0] - gwl2 ;
       assign b = sop_b_result[6:0] - bwl2 ;

    (7-bit minus 7-bit into a 7-bit target: the borrow out is dropped, which is the mod-128 arithmetic the derivation uses, matching the original's truncation of its 15-bit sum.)

  3. Change nothing else in the file; no other module is touched.

Expected effect. Area: one DSP tile less than the kept candidate (−253779) and a net loss of soft state (wl 8 FF, i1*,i2*,i3* product registers, the 8-bit subtract chain, one 15-bit add chain per colour go; 21 FF of second-rank w delay and 21 BLEs of input subtract come), so roughly 4.45e6–4.55e6 MWTA against the kept 4.73253e6 and the baseline 4.7578e6. Delay: the slowest stage is untouched, so the period should stay near the kept build's 4.57484 ns; at that period the objective lands near 2.04e7–2.08e7, below the kept 2.16506e7 and about 10 % below the baseline's 2.30616e7 — a structural tile saving, larger than the ±4.6 % placement-seed noise on this design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the measured critical path is a wide soft-mapped multiply outside bilinearintrp (matmult's am11…am33, raygentop.v:335-343, of which only some fit the DSPs in two_mult_18x19 mode); pairing those products onto int_sop_2 blocks — or raising the flow's hard-multiplier budget so none is soft-mapped — is what would shorten the design's slowest stage. Those products are not among the candidate regions offered here.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

APPROVE

Verification performed against /work/raygentop.v and sim_reference/:

Design claims — all check out.

  • bilinearintrp is raygentop.v:2762-2887; registers load unconditionally at posedge clk (2860-2883), no reset port (2762 port list) — so reset=1'b0 is correct.
  • wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (2862) is loaded at the same edge as ul<=u, vl<=v (2863-2864), so wl ≡ 255 − ul − vl (mod 256) holds at the product stage — the fold's premise.
  • r,g,b are output[6:0] (2786-2791) and assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) (2884) truncates to 7 bits, so only mod-128 is observable.
  • Visibility: ul,vl,wl,r/g/b ul/vl/wl laddered regs and i1*,i2*,i3* are declared 2796-2816 and read only at 2862-2886. The ul/vl at 2706-2756 are a different module's registers (that module ends at 2761). blr,blg,blb are read at 2011 (linearmap, blb/blg only) and 2265-2267 / 2423-2425 / 2482-2484 — reproduced bit-exactly.
  • ru…bw = shadedata[*], and assign shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in (676), a primary input (2012 port mapping confirmed).
  • matmult products at 335-343 in the DESIGN_CHANGE note exist as cited.

Algebra — exact. r ≡ ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl ≡ ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) − rwl (mod 128), using 255 ≡ −1 and that a mod-256 wl is congruent mod 128. The 7-bit wrapping input differences perturb each product by a multiple of 128 only, so result[6:0] is exact mod 128; max value 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2^37, so the block never wraps. Spot-checked numerically (incl. wl wrapping past 256 and difference underflow): ul=130,vl=200,rul=3,rvl=9,rwl=11 → ref 85, rewrite 85; ul=255,vl=255,rwl=127 → both 127; the impulse case at tb line 173 → both 127.

Timing of the correction. rwl2 (2-deep rw delay) aligns with the block's 2-stage result, matching the original's rwl at the product stage — latency unchanged at 2 edges, so r,g,b move on the same cycle.

Power-up. Block registers and the added rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are defined from the second edge; the testbench applies 8 zero lead-in cycles before cmp = 1 (tb_equiv_bilin.v:216-219), and with zero inputs the settled value is 0, matching the reference. No reliance on added-register initial values.

Ports and widths. {10'b0,u}→18, {12'b0,diff}→19 for both product pairs; chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, clk, reset all connected; no chainout reader; no block drives another.

Required items all stated: every candidate region accounted for (all nine converted, none left), BLOCKS 3 / TILES 6, longest block series in a stage (one, 2.219 ns vs 4.57484 ns), soft logic leaving each stage with its non-slowest status, and register stages before/after (2 → 2). Scope is confined to bilinearintrp; no ASSUMES: line is needed since the result holds for all input values.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
