prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349,$350), g ($351,$352,$353), b ($354,$355,$356)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349,$350), g ($351,$352,$353), b ($354,$355,$356)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.57484
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(ru-rw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(rv-rw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; r = result[6:0] - rwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; g = result[6:0] - gwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; b = result[6:0] - bwl2; chainout unused.
    (u,v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace ul,vl. ru…bw are the module's 7-bit inputs; each difference is a 7-bit wrapping subtract in soft logic, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl. rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 are rw,gw,bw delayed two stages.)

PLAN:

What changes against the kept candidate. The kept rewrite put six of the nine products in three blocks and left the three wl* products on a fourth DSP tile, so bilinearintrp went 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7; area fell only 0.53 %. This revision folds the third product of each colour into the same two block products algebraically, so all nine candidate products disappear from soft/DSP multiply logic, bilinearintrp uses 3 tiles (the three blocks) and the design is back at the baseline's 6 tiles. The extra 253779 MWTA tile of the kept candidate is given back, and the wl register, the rwl/gwl/bwl-fed products, the i3* product registers and the 8-bit 255-u-v chain go away as well; the cost is three 7-bit delay registers and three 7-bit input subtracts.

The algebra (exact, not an approximation). In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) all registers load at one posedge clk (2860-2883, no reset): ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'b11111111-u-v, rul<=ru, rwl<=rw …, and at the same edge i1r<=ul*rul, i2r<=vl*rvl, i3r<=wl*rwl. The outputs are combinational and 7 bits wide: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r declared [6:0] (2884-2886, 2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits are observable. Therefore, with every signal taken at the same register stage,

r ≡ ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl (mod 128).

wl is the 8-bit wrap of 255−u−v, and a mod-256 value is congruent mod 128, so wl·rwl ≡ (255−ul−vl)·rwl (mod 128), giving

r ≡ 255·rwl + ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) ≡ ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) − rwl (mod 128),

since 255·rwl ≡ 127·rwl ≡ −rwl (mod 128). The two remaining products are exactly ax*ay + bx*by. The differences are fed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts, which are congruent to the true differences mod 128; because a factor congruent mod 128 changes the product only by a multiple of 128 (u ≤ 255 so u·2⁷·k is a multiple of 128), and because u·(ru−rw mod 128) + v·(rv−rw mod 128) ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2³⁷ so the block never wraps, result[6:0] equals (ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl)) mod 128 exactly. The final 7-bit subtract of the delayed rw closes it. Same reasoning for g and b. No ASSUMES: line is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit differences into 19-bit ay/by).

Check on the testbench's impulse (tb_equiv_bilin.v:173, all inputs 1, selectuv=1): reference ul=vl=1, wl=253, r=(1+1+253) mod 128=127; rewrite result=1·0+1·0=0, rwl2=1, r=(0−1) mod 128=127. ✓

Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl,i1*,i2*,i3* are module-local and read only inside these three sums (they are declared at 2794-2816 and used only at 2862-2886; the ul/vl at 2752-2756 are different registers in a different module). Nothing outside can observe them, so they may be absorbed. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → blr,blg,blb → the temp_shadedata* case muxes at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484, and blb,blg → linearmap, 2011); they are reproduced bit-exactly.

Every candidate region. All nine are converted, in three groups of three, one group per colour sum; none is left. The three that the kept candidate left — $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353, $356 — are converted not by giving them a block port (a block holds only two products, and chaining is impossible here, see below) but by substituting wl = 255−u−v so that their contribution becomes part of the two products of the same block plus a 7-bit constant-times-signal correction outside it. Their multiplies disappear from the design.

Why not chaining. A second block per colour taking the first's chainout would put the sum 3 edges after the operands (chainout is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches result two edges later), while the design produces r,g,b 2 edges after the operands; it would also cost 3 more tiles. The algebraic fold gives the same result with no extra latency and no extra tile, so every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

Blocks and tiles. 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. bilinearintrp held nine products on 3 tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt; it now holds no multiply at all, so it is 3 tiles of blocks: design total 6 of 132, the baseline count. Against the kept candidate this removes one DSP tile (−253779 MWTA ≈ 9 CLBs).

Soft logic, by stage, and the slowest stage.

  • Slowest stage: untouched. The measured critical path of the kept build (4.57484 ns at seed 1) runs from a $dffe~387 through a wide soft adder array (cell_hard_block$…, stride-17 adder rows) and three LUTs into another block's register — a soft-mapped wide multiply, not in bilinearintrp (this module's multiplies are all on DSPs/blocks). This rewrite changes nothing on it, so STAGE_NS is that figure; the baseline's own slowest stage (the two_mult_18x19 hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, 4.869 on the baseline report) is likewise untouched.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ax/bx: source register → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v (2818-2858) → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 0.060+0.18+1.419+0.019 ≈ 1.7 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ay/by: ru…bw are shadedata[*] = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so the added 7-bit subtract sits on an I/O path, which is not timed. The only timed logic it could meet is none. Not the slowest.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft subtract level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. Depth is the same as the kept candidate's 7-bit add and one adder level less than the baseline's two 15-bit chains. Not the slowest.
  • New reg-to-reg stage rwl → rwl2 (and gwl,bwl): clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire + setup ≈ 1.4 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the stage that ended at the old operand/product registers — ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl,wl and the 8-bit 255−u−v carry chain, plus the i1*,i2*,i3* product registers (the rwl,gwl,bwl registers stay and gain a second rank); out of the output stage — both 15-bit add chains per colour, replaced by one 7-bit subtract. Neither stage is the slowest, which is why the gain is expected in area, with the period staying where the kept build put it.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block, 2.219 ns (input register → output register), well under 4.575 ns. No block drives another.

Register stages, per converted region. Before: operand register (ul/rul, vl/rvl, wl/rwl) then product register (i1r,i2r,i3r) — 2 stages from u,v,ru,rv,rw to the value entering the sum, and r combinational after that. After: block input register then block result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them; the −rw correction is matched by the 2-rank rw→rwl→rwl2 delay, so it is also 2 stages and r is still combinational on top. Operands present before edge k give r after edge k+1 in both designs; no cycle moves.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and its registers load unconditionally, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks and they load at every edge, matching. The block registers and the new rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench's 8 zero lead-in cycles precede any comparison (tb_equiv_bilin.v:217-219), and with zero inputs the defined value is 0, the reference's value.

Edits for the editor (only inside bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2887; do not add a module definition for int_sop_2, the primitive/behavioural model is supplied):

  1. Replace the declarations at 2796-2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1b,i2b,i3b, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1r,i2r,i3r, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl) with:

    verilog
           reg[6:0] rwl;  reg[6:0] gwl;  reg[6:0] bwl;
       reg[6:0] rwl2; reg[6:0] gwl2; reg[6:0] bwl2;

wire[6:0] rud = ru - rw; wire[6:0] rvd = rv - rw;
wire[6:0] gud = gu - gw; wire[6:0] gvd = gv - gw;
wire[6:0] bud = bu - bw; wire[6:0] bvd = bv - bw;

wire[36:0] sop_r_result, sop_g_result, sop_b_result;
wire[36:0] sop_r_chainout, sop_g_chainout, sop_b_chainout;

   Keep `reg[7:0] u;` and `reg[7:0] v;` (2794-2795) and the whole combinational mux block 2818-2858 unchanged.
2. Replace the body of the `always @(posedge clk)` block (2860-2883) with exactly:
```verilog
           rwl <= rw ; gwl <= gw ; bwl <= bw ;
           rwl2 <= rwl ; gwl2 <= gwl ; bwl2 <= bwl ;

  1. Instantiate the three blocks, connecting by name, every input port connected:

    verilog
           int_sop_2 sop_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),

    (7-bit minus 7-bit into a 7-bit target: the borrow out is dropped, which is the mod-128 arithmetic the derivation uses, matching the original's truncation of its 15-bit sum.)

  3. Change nothing else in the file; no other module is touched.

Expected effect. Area: one DSP tile less than the kept candidate (−253779) and a net loss of soft state (wl 8 FF, i1*,i2*,i3* product registers, the 8-bit subtract chain, one 15-bit add chain per colour go; 21 FF of second-rank w delay and 21 BLEs of input subtract come), so roughly 4.45e6–4.55e6 MWTA against the kept 4.73253e6 and the baseline 4.7578e6. Delay: the slowest stage is untouched, so the period should stay near the kept build's 4.57484 ns; at that period the objective lands near 2.04e7–2.08e7, below the kept 2.16506e7 and about 10 % below the baseline's 2.30616e7 — a structural tile saving, larger than the ±4.6 % placement-seed noise on this design.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

check.sh passes and the local equivalence run (my own reconstruction of the original module as bilin_ref, against the supplied testbench and block model) reports result: pass — 0 mismatches on 1052 checked cycles, no X on the DUT.

What changed in raygentop.v, module bilinearintrp

All nine regions were converted, in three groups of three — one int_sop_2 block per colour sum. None is left as a multiply: the cell census shows int_sop_2 3 and the remaining 9 $mul are all in matmult (verified per-module).

Declarations (old lines 2796–2816) — removed ul, vl, wl, i1r/i2r/i3r, i1g/i2g/i3g, i1b/i2b/i3b, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl. Kept rwl, gwl, bwl; added second-rank rwl2, gwl2, bwl2, the six 7-bit wrapping input differences (rud…bvd), and the block result/chainout wires. u, v and the selectuv mux block (2818–2858) are unchanged.

Region $348 (i1r<=ul*rul) and $349 (i2r<=vl*rvl) → the two products of sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,rud}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rvd}. The block's input registers take over ul/vl/rul/rvl, so the operand-to-output latency stays 2 edges.

Region $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl) → folded algebraically into the same block, not given a port: wl ≡ 255−u−v (mod 128), so wl·rwl ≡ −u·rw − v·rw − rw, which is absorbed into ay/by as the differences ru−rw, rv−rw plus the −rw term carried outside as rwl2. Output becomes assign r = sop_r_result[6:0] - rwl2;.

Regions $351,$352,$353 → identically on sop_g (gud, gvd, gwl2), $354,$355,$356 → on sop_b (bud, bvd, bwl2).

Clocked block (old 2860–2883) now contains only the two ranks of w delay: rwl<=rw; gwl<=gw; bwl<=bw; rwl2<=rwl; gwl2<=gwl; bwl2<=bwl;.

Block connections — every input port connected by name on all three instances: clk(clk), reset(1'b0) (the module has no reset and its registers load unconditionally), mode_sigs(12'b0), chainin(37'b0) (no chaining). chainout goes to a local wire and is read by nothing.

No ASSUMES: line is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into ax/bx; 7-bit differences into ay/by), and the block's sum tops out at 2·255·127 = 64770, far below 2³⁷, so result never wraps. No other module was touched.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/int_sop_2.v"

output
1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
14	set -u
15	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
16	here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
34	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
35	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
36	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
37	                   on {on=0}
38	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
39	  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp: one block per colour sum — r (regions $348,$349,$350), g ($351,$352,$353), b ($354,$355,$356)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.57484
MAPPING:

  • sop_r: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(ru-rw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(rv-rw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; r = result[6:0] - rwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_g: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; g = result[6:0] - gwl2; chainout unused.
  • sop_b: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)}, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, reset=1'b0, clk=clk; b = result[6:0] - bwl2; chainout unused.
    (u,v are the combinational mux outputs of raygentop.v:2818-2858, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace ul,vl. ru…bw are the module's 7-bit inputs; each difference is a 7-bit wrapping subtract in soft logic, taken unregistered — the block's input registers replace rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl. rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 are rw,gw,bw delayed two stages.)

PLAN:

What changes against the kept candidate. The kept rewrite put six of the nine products in three blocks and left the three wl* products on a fourth DSP tile, so bilinearintrp went 3 → 4 tiles and the design 6 → 7; area fell only 0.53 %. This revision folds the third product of each colour into the same two block products algebraically, so all nine candidate products disappear from soft/DSP multiply logic, bilinearintrp uses 3 tiles (the three blocks) and the design is back at the baseline's 6 tiles. The extra 253779 MWTA tile of the kept candidate is given back, and the wl register, the rwl/gwl/bwl-fed products, the i3* product registers and the 8-bit 255-u-v chain go away as well; the cost is three 7-bit delay registers and three 7-bit input subtracts.

The algebra (exact, not an approximation). In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887) all registers load at one posedge clk (2860-2883, no reset): ul<=u, vl<=v, wl<=8'b11111111-u-v, rul<=ru, rwl<=rw …, and at the same edge i1r<=ul*rul, i2r<=vl*rvl, i3r<=wl*rwl. The outputs are combinational and 7 bits wide: assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) with r declared [6:0] (2884-2886, 2786-2791), so only the low 7 bits are observable. Therefore, with every signal taken at the same register stage,

r ≡ ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl (mod 128).

wl is the 8-bit wrap of 255−u−v, and a mod-256 value is congruent mod 128, so wl·rwl ≡ (255−ul−vl)·rwl (mod 128), giving

r ≡ 255·rwl + ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) ≡ ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl) − rwl (mod 128),

since 255·rwl ≡ 127·rwl ≡ −rwl (mod 128). The two remaining products are exactly ax*ay + bx*by. The differences are fed as 7-bit wrapping subtracts, which are congruent to the true differences mod 128; because a factor congruent mod 128 changes the product only by a multiple of 128 (u ≤ 255 so u·2⁷·k is a multiple of 128), and because u·(ru−rw mod 128) + v·(rv−rw mod 128) ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2³⁷ so the block never wraps, result[6:0] equals (ul·(rul−rwl) + vl·(rvl−rwl)) mod 128 exactly. The final 7-bit subtract of the delayed rw closes it. Same reasoning for g and b. No ASSUMES: line is needed: every operand is unsigned and fits its port after zero-extension (8-bit u,v into 18-bit ax/bx; 7-bit differences into 19-bit ay/by).

Check on the testbench's impulse (tb_equiv_bilin.v:173, all inputs 1, selectuv=1): reference ul=vl=1, wl=253, r=(1+1+253) mod 128=127; rewrite result=1·0+1·0=0, rwl2=1, r=(0−1) mod 128=127. ✓

Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl,i1*,i2*,i3* are module-local and read only inside these three sums (they are declared at 2794-2816 and used only at 2862-2886; the ul/vl at 2752-2756 are different registers in a different module). Nothing outside can observe them, so they may be absorbed. r,g,b leave the module (raygentop.v:2012 → blr,blg,blb → the temp_shadedata* case muxes at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484, and blb,blg → linearmap, 2011); they are reproduced bit-exactly.

Every candidate region. All nine are converted, in three groups of three, one group per colour sum; none is left. The three that the kept candidate left — $350 (i3r<=wl*rwl), $353, $356 — are converted not by giving them a block port (a block holds only two products, and chaining is impossible here, see below) but by substituting wl = 255−u−v so that their contribution becomes part of the two products of the same block plus a 7-bit constant-times-signal correction outside it. Their multiplies disappear from the design.

Why not chaining. A second block per colour taking the first's chainout would put the sum 3 edges after the operands (chainout is registered at the second block's chainin and reaches result two edges later), while the design produces r,g,b 2 edges after the operands; it would also cost 3 more tiles. The algebraic fold gives the same result with no extra latency and no extra tile, so every chainin is tied to 0 and every chainout is left unconnected.

Blocks and tiles. 3 int_sop_2 blocks, each taking a DSP tile alone. bilinearintrp held nine products on 3 tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt; it now holds no multiply at all, so it is 3 tiles of blocks: design total 6 of 132, the baseline count. Against the kept candidate this removes one DSP tile (−253779 MWTA ≈ 9 CLBs).

Soft logic, by stage, and the slowest stage.

  • Slowest stage: untouched. The measured critical path of the kept build (4.57484 ns at seed 1) runs from a $dffe~387 through a wide soft adder array (cell_hard_block$…, stride-17 adder rows) and three LUTs into another block's register — a soft-mapped wide multiply, not in bilinearintrp (this module's multiplies are all on DSPs/blocks). This rewrite changes nothing on it, so STAGE_NS is that figure; the baseline's own slowest stage (the two_mult_18x19 hard multiply into a flip-flop in matmult, 4.869 on the baseline report) is likewise untouched.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ax/bx: source register → the selectuv mux LUT for u/v (2818-2858) → wire to the DSP (≤1.419) → block setup (0.019) ≈ 0.060+0.18+1.419+0.019 ≈ 1.7 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Stage ending at a block input, ay/by: ru…bw are shadedata[*] = tm3_sram_data_in, a primary input (raygentop.v:676, 2012), so the added 7-bit subtract sits on an I/O path, which is not timed. The only timed logic it could meet is none. Not the slowest.
  • Stage leaving a block: result clock-to-Q (0.060) → wire (≤1.314) → one 7-bit soft subtract level (≤1.240) → the temp_shadedata* case mux LUTs → flip-flop ≈ 3.8 ns worst case. Depth is the same as the kept candidate's 7-bit add and one adder level less than the baseline's two 15-bit chains. Not the slowest.
  • New reg-to-reg stage rwl → rwl2 (and gwl,bwl): clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire + setup ≈ 1.4 ns. Not the slowest.
  • Soft logic that leaves: out of the stage that ended at the old operand/product registers — ul,vl,rul,rvl,gul,gvl,bul,bvl,wl and the 8-bit 255−u−v carry chain, plus the i1*,i2*,i3* product registers (the rwl,gwl,bwl registers stay and gain a second rank); out of the output stage — both 15-bit add chains per colour, replaced by one 7-bit subtract. Neither stage is the slowest, which is why the gain is expected in area, with the period staying where the kept build put it.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one block, 2.219 ns (input register → output register), well under 4.575 ns. No block drives another.

Register stages, per converted region. Before: operand register (ul/rul, vl/rvl, wl/rwl) then product register (i1r,i2r,i3r) — 2 stages from u,v,ru,rv,rw to the value entering the sum, and r combinational after that. After: block input register then block result register — still 2 stages, with the two products and their addition between them; the −rw correction is matched by the 2-rank rw→rwl→rwl2 delay, so it is also 2 stages and r is still combinational on top. Operands present before edge k give r after edge k+1 in both designs; no cycle moves.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and its registers load unconditionally, so reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks and they load at every edge, matching. The block registers and the new rwl2,gwl2,bwl2 start unknown and are defined from the second edge; the testbench's 8 zero lead-in cycles precede any comparison (tb_equiv_bilin.v:217-219), and with zero inputs the defined value is 0, the reference's value.

Edits for the editor (only inside bilinearintrp, raygentop.v:2762-2887; do not add a module definition for int_sop_2, the primitive/behavioural model is supplied):

  1. Replace the declarations at 2796-2816 (ul, vl, wl, i1b,i2b,i3b, i1g,i2g,i3g, i1r,i2r,i3r, rul,rvl,rwl, gul,gvl,gwl, bul,bvl,bwl) with:

    verilog
           reg[6:0] rwl;  reg[6:0] gwl;  reg[6:0] bwl;
       reg[6:0] rwl2; reg[6:0] gwl2; reg[6:0] bwl2;

wire[6:0] rud = ru - rw; wire[6:0] rvd = rv - rw;
wire[6:0] gud = gu - gw; wire[6:0] gvd = gv - gw;
wire[6:0] bud = bu - bw; wire[6:0] bvd = bv - bw;

wire[36:0] sop_r_result, sop_g_result, sop_b_result;
wire[36:0] sop_r_chainout, sop_g_chainout, sop_b_chainout;

   Keep `reg[7:0] u;` and `reg[7:0] v;` (2794-2795) and the whole combinational mux block 2818-2858 unchanged.
2. Replace the body of the `always @(posedge clk)` block (2860-2883) with exactly:
```verilog
           rwl <= rw ; gwl <= gw ; bwl <= bw ;
           rwl2 <= rwl ; gwl2 <= gwl ; bwl2 <= bwl ;

  1. Instantiate the three blocks, connecting by name, every input port connected:

    verilog
           int_sop_2 sop_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
           .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, rud}), .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, rvd}),

  2. Replace 2884-2886 with:

    verilog
           assign r = sop_r_result[6:0] - rwl2 ;
       assign g = sop_g_result[6:0] - gwl2 ;
       assign b = sop_b_result[6:0] - bwl2 ;

    (7-bit minus 7-bit into a 7-bit target: the borrow out is dropped, which is the mod-128 arithmetic the derivation uses, matching the original's truncation of its 15-bit sum.)

  3. Change nothing else in the file; no other module is touched.

Expected effect. Area: one DSP tile less than the kept candidate (−253779) and a net loss of soft state (wl 8 FF, i1*,i2*,i3* product registers, the 8-bit subtract chain, one 15-bit add chain per colour go; 21 FF of second-rank w delay and 21 BLEs of input subtract come), so roughly 4.45e6–4.55e6 MWTA against the kept 4.73253e6 and the baseline 4.7578e6. Delay: the slowest stage is untouched, so the period should stay near the kept build's 4.57484 ns; at that period the objective lands near 2.04e7–2.08e7, below the kept 2.16506e7 and about 10 % below the baseline's 2.30616e7 — a structural tile saving, larger than the ±4.6 % placement-seed noise on this design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the measured critical path is a wide soft-mapped multiply outside bilinearintrp (matmult's am11…am33, raygentop.v:335-343, of which only some fit the DSPs in two_mult_18x19 mode); pairing those products onto int_sop_2 blocks — or raising the flow's hard-multiplier budget so none is soft-mapped — is what would shorten the design's slowest stage. Those products are not among the candidate regions offered here.

Simulation

simulation passed: 1052 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows that every output of bilinearintrp matches on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles); it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the source and supporting files.

ACCEPT

Plan conformance. The edit is exactly the five steps of the approved plan, all inside bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2794-2866): the dead declarations replaced by rwl/gwl/bwl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, the six 7-bit difference wires and the result/chainout wires (2796-2804); the always @(posedge clk) body reduced to the two delay ranks (2848-2852); the three instances (2854-2862); the three output assigns (2864-2866). u/v and the selectuv mux block (2806-2846) are untouched, no int_sop_2 module definition was added, and the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is byte-equal to the original.

Ports. All nine regions are gone — no multiply remains in the module. Each instance connects every input port by name: clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u} (18), ay={12'b0,·d} (19), bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,·d}, chainin=37'b0 (a constant, permitted on a dedicated chain input). result[6:0] drives only the r/g/b assigns; chainout goes to local wires that nothing reads, so no one else observes it — no block drives another.

Correctness.

  • Behaviour. Original: r = (ul·rul + vl·rvl + wl·rwl) truncated to 7 bits, with wl the 8-bit wrap of 255−u−v. Mod 128, wl·rwl ≡ (255−u−v)·rw, so r ≡ u·(ru−rw) + v·(rv−rw) − rw. The 7-bit wrapping differences rud…bvd differ from the true differences by multiples of 128, and u,v are integers, so each product is unchanged mod 128. The block's sum u·rud + v·rvd ≤ 2·255·127 = 64770 < 2³⁷ never wraps, so result[6:0] is that sum mod 128; the 7-bit − rwl2 closes it in mod-128 arithmetic (both operands and the target are 7 bits, so the borrow is dropped as the original dropped its 15-bit truncation). Exact, not approximate, for every input.
  • Latency. Original: operand rank (ul,rwl,wl) at edge k, product rank at k+1, r combinational. Rewrite per int_sop_2.v:37-46: input registers at edge k, result at k+1, r combinational. rwl2 after edge k+1 holds rw from before edge k, matching the stage of the original's rwl feeding i3r. Same cycle on both.
  • Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul…bwl,i1*,i2*,i3* were read only at 2862-2886 of the original; the grep confirms the only other ul/vl are separate registers in the module ending at raygentop.v:2761. r,g,b are reproduced bit-exactly.
  • Reset / power-up. The module has no reset and loads unconditionally, so reset=1'b0 matches at every edge, not just start-up. The block registers and rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 start unknown and are defined from the second edge; nothing reads the result earlier (x_on_dut_only = 0).
  • Assumptions. None required, and none claimed — every operand fits its port zero-extended, and the bound above holds for all inputs, so the testbench's unconstrained stimulus is covered.
  • Scope. 3 blocks on 3 DSP tiles of 132; nothing outside the named regions changed.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 2.30616e+07 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.60%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
